{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01GT3rj6EbcH28kPFEnvDhP2","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01AcEB4B4T156pTgzfLdWgyW","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/scala-generics-and-variance.md","content":"# Scala Generics and Type Variance\n\n## Overview\nType variance in Scala allows for flexible and safe generic programming. Understanding covariance, contravariance, and invariance is essential for writing idiomatic Scala code.\n\n## Scala 2.13 Setup\n```scala\n// Scala version requirement\nscalaVersion := \"2.13.x\"\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### 1. Variance Annotations\n- **Covariance (+T)**: Allows subtype relationships to be preserved. Used for \"producer\" types.\n- **Contravariance (-T)**: Reverses subtype relationships. Used for \"consumer\" types.\n- **Invariance (T)**: No subtype relationship preservation. Used for types that are both producers and consumers.\n\n### 2. Covariant Container (Producer)\n```scala\n// Covariant - produces values of type T\nclass Container[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  def get: Seq[T] = items\n  def map[U](f: T => U): Container[U] = new Container(items.map(f))\n  // CANNOT have methods that accept T as input (Consumer)\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intContainer: Container[Int] = new Container(Seq(1, 2, 3))\nval numberContainer: Container[Number] = intContainer // OK because Container is covariant\n```\n\n### 3. Contravariant Container (Consumer)\n```scala\n// Contravariant - consumes values of type T\ntrait Consumer[-T] {\n  def consume(value: T): Unit\n}\n\n// Implementation\nclass NumberConsumer extends Consumer[Number] {\n  def consume(value: Number): Unit = println(value)\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intConsumer: Consumer[Int] = new NumberConsumer() // OK because Consumer is contravariant\n```\n\n### 4. Invariant Container (Producer and Consumer)\n```scala\n// Invariant - both produces and consumes T\nclass Box[T](var value: T) {\n  def get: T = value\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = { value = newValue }\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intBox: Box[Int] = new Box(5)\n// val numberBox: Box[Number] = intBox // NOT OK - invariant type\n```\n\n### 5. Type Bounds\n```scala\n// Upper bound: T must be a subtype of Comparable\nclass Comparator[T <: Comparable[T]] {\n  def compare(a: T, b: T): Int = a.compareTo(b)\n}\n\n// Lower bound: T must be a supertype of Int\nclass Processor[-T >: Int] {\n  def process(value: T): Unit = println(value)\n}\n\n// Context bound: requires implicit Ordering[T]\nclass Sorter[T: Ordering](items: Seq[T]) {\n  def sort: Seq[T] = items.sorted\n}\n```\n\n### 6. Type Classes for Dynamic Dispatch\n```scala\n// Define a type class\ntrait Tokenizable[T] {\n  def toToken(value: T): String\n}\n\n// Implicit instances\nimplicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] =\n  new Tokenizable[Int] {\n    def toToken(value: Int): String = value.toString\n  }\n\nimplicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] =\n  new Tokenizable[String] {\n    def toToken(value: String): String = value\n  }\n\n// Use the type class\ndef tokenize[T](value: T)(implicit tokenizer: Tokenizable[T]): String =\n  tokenizer.toToken(value)\n\n// Or with context bound syntax\ndef tokenizeCtx[T: Tokenizable](value: T): String =\n  implicitly[Tokenizable[T]].toToken(value)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- Use covariance for \"return types\" and immutable collections\n- Use contravariance for \"parameter types\" and callbacks\n- Use invariance when your type needs both input and output\n- Type bounds constrain generic types to work with specific operations\n- Type classes enable ad-hoc polymorphism without tight coupling\n\n## Common Patterns\n- **Producer extends Container[+T]**: Collections that produce values\n- **Consumer extends [-T]**: Callbacks and handlers that accept values\n- **Handler[T]**: Invariant when you need both input and output\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":309,"cache_read_input_tokens":25630,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":309,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9dc3a83c-7db6-4924-a861-5c4670514295","uuid":"d846145a-d23f-40f2-b75f-a42ad3a42bdc"}

Understanding covariance, contravariance, and invariance is essential for writing idiomatic Scala code.\n\n## Scala 2.13 Setup\n```scala\n// Scala version requirement\nscalaVersion := \"2.13.x\"\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### 1. Variance Annotations\n- **Covariance (+T)**: Allows subtype relationships to be preserved. Used for \"producer\" types.\n- **Contravariance (-T)**: Reverses subtype relationships. Used for \"consumer\" types.\n- **Invariance (T)**: No subtype relationship preservation. Used for types that are both producers and consumers.\n\n### 2. Covariant Container (Producer)\n```scala\n// Covariant - produces values of type T\nclass Container[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  def get: Seq[T] = items\n  def map[U](f: T => U): Container[U] = new Container(items.map(f))\n  // CANNOT have methods that accept T as input (Consumer)\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intContainer: Container[Int] = new Container(Seq(1, 2, 3))\nval numberContainer: Container[Number] = intContainer // OK because Container is covariant\n```\n\n### 3. Contravariant Container (Consumer)\n```scala\n// Contravariant - consumes values of type T\ntrait Consumer[-T] {\n  def consume(value: T): Unit\n}\n\n// Implementation\nclass NumberConsumer extends Consumer[Number] {\n  def consume(value: Number): Unit = println(value)\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intConsumer: Consumer[Int] = new NumberConsumer() // OK because Consumer is contravariant\n```\n\n### 4. Invariant Container (Producer and Consumer)\n```scala\n// Invariant - both produces and consumes T\nclass Box[T](var value: T) {\n  def get: T = value\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = { value = newValue }\n}\n\n// Usage\nval intBox: Box[Int] = new Box(5)\n// val numberBox: Box[Number] = intBox // NOT OK - invariant type\n```\n\n### 5. Type Bounds\n```scala\n// Upper bound: T must be a subtype of Comparable\nclass Comparator[T <: Comparable[T]] {\n  def compare(a: T, b: T): Int = a.compareTo(b)\n}\n\n// Lower bound: T must be a supertype of Int\nclass Processor[-T >: Int] {\n  def process(value: T): Unit = println(value)\n}\n\n// Context bound: requires implicit Ordering[T]\nclass Sorter[T: Ordering](items: Seq[T]) {\n  def sort: Seq[T] = items.sorted\n}\n```\n\n### 6. Type Classes for Dynamic Dispatch\n```scala\n// Define a type class\ntrait Tokenizable[T] {\n  def toToken(value: T): String\n}\n\n// Implicit instances\nimplicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] =\n  new Tokenizable[Int] {\n    def toToken(value: Int): String = value.toString\n  }\n\nimplicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] =\n  new Tokenizable[String] {\n    def toToken(value: String): String = value\n  }\n\n// Use the type class\ndef tokenize[T](value: T)(implicit tokenizer: Tokenizable[T]): String =\n  tokenizer.toToken(value)\n\n// Or with context bound syntax\ndef tokenizeCtx[T: Tokenizable](value: T): String =\n  implicitly[Tokenizable[T]].toToken(value)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- Use covariance for \"return types\" and immutable collections\n- Use contravariance for \"parameter types\" and callbacks\n- Use invariance when your type needs both input and output\n- Type bounds constrain generic types to work with specific operations\n- Type classes enable ad-hoc polymorphism without tight coupling\n\n## Common Patterns\n- **Producer extends Container[+T]**: Collections that produce values\n- **Consumer extends [-T]**: Callbacks and handlers that accept values\n- **Handler[T]**: Invariant when you need both input and output\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Immutable Data Representation\n```scala\n// Simple case class - immutable by default\ncase class Token(value: String, tokenType: TokenType, metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty) {\n  def withMetadata(newMetadata: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    this.copy(metadata = metadata ++ newMetadata)\n}\n\n// Case classes provide:\n// - equals(), hashCode(), toString() automatically\n// - copy() method for creating modified copies\n// - Pattern matching support\n```\n\n### 2. Copy Method for Immutability\n```scala\nval token1 = Token(\"hello\", TokenType.STRING)\n// Create new token with modified metadata\nval token2 = token1.copy(metadata = token1.metadata + (\"source\" -> \"user\"))\n// Original token1 is unchanged\n```\n\n## Sealed Traits and Sum Types\n\n### 1. Sealed Trait Hierarchy\n```scala\n// Sealed trait ensures exhaustiveness checking in pattern matches\nsealed trait TokenType\nobject TokenType {\n  case object STRING extends TokenType\n  case object NUMERIC extends TokenType\n  case object TEMPORAL extends TokenType\n  case object STRUCTURED extends TokenType\n  case object BINARY extends TokenType\n  case object NULL extends TokenType\n}\n\n// Enum-like pattern (alternative to Enum class)\nsealed abstract class TokenTypeEnum(val value: String)\nobject TokenTypeEnum {\n  case object STRING extends TokenTypeEnum(\"string\")\n  case object NUMERIC extends TokenTypeEnum(\"numeric\")\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Exhaustive Pattern Matching\n```scala\ndef processToken(token: Token): String = token.tokenType match {\n  case TokenType.STRING => s\"String: ${token.value}\"\n  case TokenType.NUMERIC => s\"Number: ${token.value}\"\n  case TokenType.TEMPORAL => s\"Date: ${token.value}\"\n  case TokenType.STRUCTURED => s\"Struct: ${token.value}\"\n  case TokenType.BINARY => s\"Binary: ${token.value}\"\n  case TokenType.NULL => \"NULL\"\n  // Compiler ensures all cases are covered\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Recursive Algebraic Data Types\n```scala\n// For representing JSON-like structures\nsealed trait JsonValue\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Vector[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(fields: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\n\n// Pattern matching on recursive types\ndef toToken(json: JsonValue): String = json match {\n  case JsonString(s) => s\n  case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n  case JsonArray(arr) => arr.map(toToken).mkString(\"[\", \", \", \"]\")\n  case JsonObject(fields) =>\n    fields.map { case (k, v) => s\"$k: ${toToken(v)}\" }.mkString(\"{\", \", \", \"}\")\n  case JsonNull => \"null\"\n}\n```\n\n## Option and Either\n\n### 1. Optional Values with Option\n```scala\n// Instead of null or Maybe types\nsealed trait Option[+A]\ncase class Some[A](value: A) extends Option[A]\ncase object None extends Option[Nothing]\n\n// Usage\ndef findToken(id: String): Option[Token] = ???\n\n// Pattern matching\nfindToken(\"123\") match {\n  case Some(token) => println(s\"Found: $token\")\n  case None => println(\"Not found\")\n}\n\n// Functional operations\nfindToken(\"123\")\n  .map(token => token.withMetadata(\"processed\" -> true))\n  .getOrElse(Token(\"default\", TokenType.NULL))\n```\n\n### 2. Basic Try Usage\n```scala\nimport scala.util.{Try, Success, Failure}\n\n// Wraps computations that might throw exceptions\ndef parseNumber(str: String): Try[Int] = Try(str.toInt)\n\n// Pattern matching\nparseNumber(\"123\") match {\n  case Success(num) => println(s\"Parsed: $num\")\n  case Failure(ex) => println(s\"Error: ${ex.getMessage}\")\n}\n\n// Functional operations\nparseNumber(\"123\")\n  .map(_ * 2)\n  .getOrElse(0)\n```\n\n### 2. Chaining Try Operations\n```scala\ndef tokenizeSafe(value: String): Try[Token] = {\n  Try {\n    val trimmed = value.trim\n    if (trimmed.isEmpty) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Empty value\")\n    Token(trimmed, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n}\n\n// Chaining with flatMap\ndef processAndValidate(raw: String): Try[Token] = {\n  tokenizeSafe(raw).flatMap { token =>\n    if (token.value.length > 1000) {\n      Failure(new IllegalArgumentException(\"Token too long\"))\n    } else {\n      Success(token)\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Recovering from Failures\n```scala\nval result = parseNumber(\"invalid\")\n  .recover { case _: NumberFormatException => 0 }\n  .recoverWith { case ex => Failure(new RuntimeException(\"Custom error\", ex)) }\n\n// Or with getOrElse\nval value = parseNumber(\"invalid\").getOrElse(-1)\n```\n\n## Option - Absence Handling\n\n### 1. Using Option Instead of Null\n```scala\ndef findTokenByValue(value: String): Option[Token] = {\n  // Instead of returning null\n  if (value.nonEmpty) Some(Token(value, TokenType.STRING))\n  else None\n}\n\n// Safe access\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\") match {\n  case Some(token) => println(token)\n  case None => println(\"Not found\")\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Option Operations\n```scala\n// map: transforms the value inside\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").map(_.value) // Option[String]\n\n// flatMap: chains operations that return Option\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\")\n  .flatMap(token => if (token.value.length > 3) Some(token) else None)\n\n// filter: keeps Some if predicate is true\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").filter(_.value.startsWith(\"t\"))\n\n// getOrElse: provide default for None\nfindTokenByValue(\"\").getOrElse(Token(\"default\", TokenType.NULL))\n\n// fold: handle both Some and None\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").fold(\"Not found\")(token => s\"Found: ${token.value}\")\n```\n\n### 3. Collections of Options\n```scala\nval tokens: Seq[Option[Token]] = Seq(\n  findTokenByValue(\"a\"),\n  findTokenByValue(\"\"),\n  findTokenByValue(\"b\")\n)\n\n// Flatten to remove None values\nval validTokens: Seq[Token] = tokens.flatten\n\n// Or use flatMap\nval processed = tokens.flatMap(identity)\n\n// Sequence to convert Option[Seq[T]] to Seq[Option[T]]\nval maybeTokens: Option[Seq[Token]] = Some(Seq(token1, token2))\nval sequenced: Seq[Option[Token]] = ???\n```\n\n## Either - Explicit Error Information\n\n### 1. Left for Errors, Right for Success\n```scala\ndef tokenizeWithError(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  value.trim match {\n    case empty if empty.isEmpty => Left(\"Value cannot be empty\")\n    case trimmed if trimmed.length > 1000 => Left(\"Value too long\")\n    case trimmed => Right(Token(trimmed, TokenType.STRING))\n  }\n}\n\n// Pattern matching\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\") match {\n  case Right(token) => println(s\"Success: $token\")\n  case Left(error) => println(s\"Error: $error\")\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Chaining Either Operations\n```scala\ndef processToken(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  tokenizeWithError(value).flatMap { token =>\n    if (token.value.contains(\" \")) {\n      Left(\"Token cannot contain spaces\")\n    } else {\n      Right(token)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// Or use for-comprehension\ndef processTokenFor(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  for {\n    token <- tokenizeWithError(value)\n    _ <- if (token.value.length >= 3) Right(()) else Left(\"Token too short\")\n  } yield token\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Converting Between Error Types\n```scala\n// Either to Option (discarding error info)\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\").toOption\n\n// Either to Try (converting error to exception)\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\").toTry\n\n// Try to Either\nTry(\"test\".toInt).toEither // Either[Throwable, Int]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### When to Use What\n- **Try**: Wrapping code that throws exceptions\n- **Option**: For truly optional values (absence vs. presence)\n- **Either**: For explicit error handling with custom error types\n\n### Idiomatic Patterns\n```scala\n// Good: Using for-comprehension for chaining\nfor {\n  token1 <- tokenizeWithError(\"test1\")\n  token2 <- tokenizeWithError(\"test2\")\n} yield (token1, token2)\n\n// Avoid: Nested pattern matching when for-comprehension works\ntokenizeWithError(\"test1\") match {\n  case Right(t1) =>\n    tokenizeWithError(\"test2\") match {\n      case Right(t2) => ???\n      case Left(e) => ???\n    }\n  case Left(e) => ???\n}\n\n// Avoid: Using Try when Option is sufficient\ndef getValue: Option[String] = ??? // Good\ndef getValue: Try[String] = ??? // Overkill if no exceptions thrown\n```\n\n## Exception Handling Strategies\n\n### 1. Basic Try Usage\n```scala\nimport scala.util.{Try, Success, Failure}\n\n// Wraps computations that might throw exceptions\ndef parseNumber(str: String): Try[Int] = Try(str.toInt)\n\n// Pattern matching\nparseNumber(\"123\") match {\n  case Success(num) => println(s\"Parsed: $num\")\n  case Failure(ex) => println(s\"Error: ${ex.getMessage}\")\n}\n\n// Functional operations\nparseNumber(\"123\")\n  .map(_ * 2)\n  .getOrElse(0)\n```\n\n### 2. Chaining Try Operations\n```scala\ndef tokenizeSafe(value: String): Try[Token] = {\n  Try {\n    val trimmed = value.trim\n    if (trimmed.isEmpty) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Empty value\")\n    Token(trimmed, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n}\n\n// Chaining with flatMap\ndef processAndValidate(raw: String): Try[Token] = {\n  tokenizeSafe(raw).flatMap { token =>\n    if (token.value.length > 1000) {\n      Failure(new IllegalArgumentException(\"Token too long\"))\n    } else {\n      Success(token)\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Recovering from Failures\n```scala\nval result = parseNumber(\"invalid\")\n  .recover { case _: NumberFormatException => 0 }\n  .recoverWith { case ex => Failure(new RuntimeException(\"Custom error\", ex)) }\n\n// Or with getOrElse\nval value = parseNumber(\"invalid\").getOrElse(-1)\n```\n\n## Option - Absence Handling\n\n### 1. Using Option Instead of Null\n```scala\ndef findTokenByValue(value: String): Option[Token] = {\n  // Instead of returning null\n  if (value.nonEmpty) Some(Token(value, TokenType.STRING))\n  else None\n}\n\n// Safe access\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\") match {\n  case Some(token) => println(token)\n  case None => println(\"Not found\")\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Option Operations\n```scala\n// map: transforms the value inside\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").map(_.value) // Option[String]\n\n// flatMap: chains operations that return Option\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\")\n  .flatMap(token => if (token.value.length > 3) Some(token) else None)\n\n// filter: keeps Some if predicate is true\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").filter(_.value.startsWith(\"t\"))\n\n// getOrElse: provide default for None\nfindTokenByValue(\"\").getOrElse(Token(\"default\", TokenType.NULL))\n\n// fold: handle both Some and None\nfindTokenByValue(\"test\").fold(\"Not found\")(token => s\"Found: ${token.value}\")\n```\n\n### 3. Collections of Options\n```scala\nval tokens: Seq[Option[Token]] = Seq(\n  findTokenByValue(\"a\"),\n  findTokenByValue(\"\"),\n  findTokenByValue(\"b\")\n)\n\n// Flatten to remove None values\nval validTokens: Seq[Token] = tokens.flatten\n\n// Or use flatMap\nval processed = tokens.flatMap(identity)\n\n// Sequence to convert Option[Seq[T]] to Seq[Option[T]]\nval maybeTokens: Option[Seq[Token]] = Some(Seq(token1, token2))\nval sequenced: Seq[Option[Token]] = ???\n```\n\n## Either - Explicit Error Information\n\n### 1. Left for Errors, Right for Success\n```scala\ndef tokenizeWithError(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  value.trim match {\n    case empty if empty.isEmpty => Left(\"Value cannot be empty\")\n    case trimmed if trimmed.length > 1000 => Left(\"Value too long\")\n    case trimmed => Right(Token(trimmed, TokenType.STRING))\n  }\n}\n\n// Pattern matching\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\") match {\n  case Right(token) => println(s\"Success: $token\")\n  case Left(error) => println(s\"Error: $error\")\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Chaining Either Operations\n```scala\ndef processToken(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  tokenizeWithError(value).flatMap { token =>\n    if (token.value.contains(\" \")) {\n      Left(\"Token cannot contain spaces\")\n    } else {\n      Right(token)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// Or use for-comprehension\ndef processTokenFor(value: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n  for {\n    token <- tokenizeWithError(value)\n    _ <- if (token.value.length >= 3) Right(()) else Left(\"Token too short\")\n  } yield token\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Converting Between Error Types\n```scala\n// Either to Option (discarding error info)\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\").toOption\n\n// Either to Try (converting error to exception)\ntokenizeWithError(\"test\").toTry\n\n// Try to Either\nTry(\"test\".toInt).toEither // Either[Throwable, Int]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### When to Use What\n- **Try**: Wrapping code that throws exceptions\n- **Option**: For truly optional values (absence vs. presence)\n- **Either**: For explicit error handling with custom error types\n\n### Idiomatic Patterns\n```scala\n// Good: Using for-comprehension for chaining\nfor {\n  token1 <- tokenizeWithError(\"test1\")\n  token2 <- tokenizeWithError(\"test2\")\n} yield (token1, token2)\n\n// Avoid: Nested pattern matching when for-comprehension works\ntokenizeWithError(\"test1\") match {\n  case Right(t1) =>\n    tokenizeWithError(\"test2\") match {\n      case Right(t2) => ???\n      case Left(e) => ???\n    }\n  case Left(e) => ???\n}\n\n// Avoid: Using Try when Option is sufficient\ndef getValue: Option[String] = ??? // Good\ndef getValue: Try[String] = ??? // Overkill if no exceptions thrown\n```\n\n## Exception Handling Strategies\n\n### 1. Preventing vs. Handling\n```scala\n// Prevent: Check before operation\ndef safeHead[T](seq: Seq[T]): Option[T] = seq.headOption\n\n// Don't handle: seq.head wrapped in Try (recoverable exceptions should be prevented)\ndef badHead[T](seq: Seq[T]): Try[T] = Try(seq.head)\n```\n\n### 2. Defining a Type Class\n```scala\n// A type class defines operations for a family of types\ntrait Show[A] {\n  def show(a: A): String\n}\n\ntrait Numeric[A] {\n  def parse(str: String): A\n  def format(a: A, precision: Int): String\n}\n\ntrait Tokenizable[A] {\n  def toToken(value: A): Token\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Implicit Instances\n```scala\n// Provide implementations for specific types\nobject Show {\n  implicit val showInt: Show[Int] = new Show[Int] {\n    def show(a: Int): String = a.toString\n  }\n\n  implicit val showString: Show[String] = new Show[String] {\n    def show(a: String): String = s\"\\\"$a\\\"\"\n  }\n\n  // Using shorthand syntax\n  implicit val showDouble: Show[Double] = a => a.toString\n}\n\n// Or using implicit conversion\nimplicit val showToken: Show[Token] = _.value\n```\n\n### 3. Using Type Classes with Implicits\n```scala\n// Implicit parameter - explicit syntax\ndef display[A](a: A)(implicit shower: Show[A]): String = shower.show(a)\n\n// Context bound - syntactic sugar\ndef display[A: Show](a: A): String = implicitly[Show[A]].show(a)\n\n// Usage\ndisplay(42) // Uses Show[Int]\ndisplay(\"hello\") // Uses Show[String]\n```\n\n### 4. Type Class Derivation\n```scala\n// Automatic derivation for product types\nimplicit def showTuple[A: Show, B: Show]: Show[(A, B)] = {\n  case (a, b) => s\"(${implicitly[Show[A]].show(a)}, ${implicitly[Show[B]].show(b)})\"\n}\n\nimplicit def showOption[A: Show]: Show[Option[A]] = {\n  case Some(a) => s\"Some(${implicitly[Show[A]].show(a)})\"\n  case None => \"None\"\n}\n\n// Usage\ndisplay((42, \"hello\")) // Derives Show[(Int, String)]\ndisplay(Some(42)) // Derives Show[Option[Int]]\n```\n\n## Higher-Order Functions and Functional Composition\n\n### 1. Function Types\n```scala\n// Basic function type\nval add: (Int, Int) => Int = (a, b) => a + b\n\n// Multiple parameter lists (currying)\nval addCurried: Int => Int => Int = a => b => a + b\n\n// Generic function\ndef map[A, B](seq: Seq[A])(f: A => B): Seq[B] = seq.map(f)\n\n// Function as return type\ndef makeMultiplier(n: Int): Int => Int = a => a * n\n```\n\n### 2. Function Composition\n```scala\n// Compose functions\nval trim: String => String = _.trim\nval lowercase: String => String = _.toLowerCase\nval composed: String => String = s => lowercase(trim(s))\n\n// Using andThen (left to right)\nval process = trim.andThen(lowercase)\n\n// Using compose (right to left)\nval process2 = lowercase.compose(trim)\n\n// Higher-level composition\ndef pipe[A, B, C](a: A)(f: A => B)(g: B => C): C = g(f(a))\n```\n\n### 3. Monadic Operations\n```scala\n// map: transform the value\nOption(5).map(_ * 2) // Some(10)\n\n// flatMap: chain operations that return Option/Try/Either\nOption(5).flatMap(n => if (n > 3) Some(n * 2) else None)\n\n// filter: keep if predicate is true\nOption(5).filter(_ > 3)\n\n// fold: handle all cases\nOption(5).fold(0)(identity)\n\n// For-comprehension (syntactic sugar for flatMap + map)\nfor {\n  x <- Option(5)\n  y <- Option(3)\n  z <- Option(2)\n} yield x + y + z\n```\n\n## Practical Type Class Examples\n\n### 1. Tokenizable Type Class\n```scala\ntrait Tokenizable[A] {\n  def toToken(value: A): Token\n}\n\n// Instances\nobject Tokenizable {\n  implicit val tokenizableString: Tokenizable[String] =\n    value => Token(value, TokenType.STRING)\n\n  implicit val tokenizableInt: Tokenizable[Int] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"type\" -> \"int\"))\n\n  implicit def tokenizableOption[A: Tokenizable]: Tokenizable[Option[A]] = {\n    case Some(a) => implicitly[Tokenizable[A]].toToken(a)\n    case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n  }\n}\n\n// Generic tokenization function\ndef tokenize[A: Tokenizable](value: A): Token =\n  implicitly[Tokenizable[A]].toToken(value)\n```\n\n### 2. Builder Pattern with Type Classes\n```scala\nclass TokenizerBuilder[A: Tokenizable] {\n  private var normalizers: List[String => String] = List.empty\n  private var validators: List[A => Boolean] = List.empty\n  private var metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n\n  def withNormalizer(f: String => String): this.type = {\n    normalizers = normalizers :+ f\n    this\n  }\n\n  def withValidator(f: A => Boolean): this.type = {\n    validators = validators :+ f\n    this\n  }\n\n  def withMetadata(data: (String, Any)*): this.type = {\n    metadata = metadata ++ data\n    this\n  }\n\n  def build(): A => Token = { value =>\n    validators.foreach { v =>\n      require(v(value), s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n    }\n\n    val tokenizer = implicitly[Tokenizable[A]]\n    var token = tokenizer.toToken(value)\n\n    normalizers.foldLeft(token.value) { (acc, norm) => norm(acc) }\n\n    token.copy(metadata = token.metadata ++ metadata)\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Practical Patterns for Distributed Systems\n\n### 1. Batch Processing\n```scala\ntrait Batchable[A] {\n  def batch(items: Seq[A]): Seq[Token]\n}\n\nimplicit val batchableSeq: Batchable[String] = items =>\n  items.map(s => Token(s, TokenType.STRING))\n\n// For Spark RDDs\ndef processBatch[A: Batchable](rdd: RDD[A]): RDD[Token] =\n  rdd.mapPartitions(partition => implicitly[Batchable[A]].batch(partition.toSeq).iterator)\n```\n\n### 2. Design Guidelines\n- Define type classes as traits with a single responsibility\n- Provide implicit instances in the companion object\n- Use context bounds for cleaner signatures\n- Leverage implicit derivation for common patterns\n\n### 2. ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Token type enumeration representing different categories of tokenized data.\n  */\nsealed abstract class  case \"structured\" => Some(STRUCTURED)\n    case \"binary\" => Some(BINARY)\n    case \"null\" => Some(NULL)\n    case _ => None\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Core Token Classes\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * =\n    this.copy(metadata = metadata ++ pairs)\n}\n\n/**\n  * Mutable batch of tokens for accumulation during processing.\n  * Contrast with immutable Token class.\n  *\n  * @param tokens initial sequence of tokens\n  * @param processed flag indicating if batch has been finalized\n  */\nclass MutableTokenBatch(\n  val tokens: scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[Token] = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer.empty,\n  private var processed: Boolean = false\n) {\n  /**\n    * Add a token to the batch.\n    *\n    * @param token token to add\n    * @throws IllegalStateException if batch is already processed\n    */\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    require(!processed, \"Batch already processed\")\n    tokens += token\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Mark the ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Covariant container that produces values of type T.\n  * Allows subtype relationships to be preserved.\n  *\n  * @param items sequence of items to store\n  * @tparam T the type of items (covariant)\n  */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  /**\n    * Get all items as an immutable sequence.\n    *\n    * @return vector of all items\n    */\n  def getAll: Vector[T] = _items\n\n  /**\n    * Map a function over all items.\n    *\n    * @param func values of type T.\n  * Allows accepting supertypes (contravariance).\n  *\n  * @tparam T the type of values (contravariant)\n  */\nclass TokenSink[-T] {\n  private val received = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer.empty[Any]\n\n  /**\n    * Receive a value into the sink.\n    *\n    * @param item item to receive\n    */\n  def receive(item: T): Unit = {\n    received += item\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Drain all received items and clear the sink.\n    *\n    * @return immutable vector of all received items\n    */\n  def drain(): Vector[Any] = {\n    val result = received.toVector\n    received.clear()\n    result\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Invariant handler that both produces and consumes values of type T.\n  * Exact type matching required.\n  *\n  * @param default initial value\n  * @tparam T the type of values (invariant)\n  */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](default: T) {\n  private var value: T = default\n\n  /**\n    * Get the current value.\n    *\n    * @return current value\n    */\n  def get: T = value\n\n  /**\n    * Set a new value.\n    *\n    * @param newValue the value to set\n    */\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = {\n    value = newValue\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Transform the current value using a function.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return the new value after transformation\n    */\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    value = func(value)\n    value\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Type Classes for Dynamic Dispatch\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Type class for converting values to tokens.\n  * Enables ad-hoc polymorphism for arbitrary types.\n  *\n  * @tparam A the type that can be tokenized\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable[A] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a value to a Token.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return the resulting Token\n    */\n  def toToken(value: A): Token\n}\n\nobject Tokenizable {\n  /**\n    * Summon an implicit Tokenizable instance for type A.\n    *\n    * @tparam A the type to tokenize\n    * @return the Tokenizable instance\n    */\n  def apply[A](implicit ev: Tokenizable[A]): Tokenizable[A] = ev\n\n  // Implicit instances for common types\n  implicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] =\n    value => Token(value, TokenType.STRING)\n\n  implicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"type\" -> \"int\"))\n\n  implicit val longTokenizable: Tokenizable[Long] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"type\" -> \"long\"))\n\n  implicit val doubleTokenizable: Tokenizable[Double] =\n    value => Token(f\"$value%.6f\", TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"type\" -> \"double\"))\n\n  implicit val bigDecimalTokenizable: Tokenizable[BigDecimal] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"type\" -> \"bigdecimal\"))\n\n  implicit val localDateTimeTokenizable: Tokenizable[LocalDateTime] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n\n  implicit val localDateTokenizable: Tokenizable[LocalDate] =\n    value => Token(value.toString, TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n\n  implicit val optionTokenizable[A: Tokenizable]: Tokenizable[Option[A]] = {\n    case Some(a) => implicitly[Tokenizable[A]].toToken(a)\n    case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Base Tokenizer Trait\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract base tokenizer with generic input type.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the input type to tokenize\n  */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return the resulting Token\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  /**\n    * Lazy tokenization of multiple values.\n    *\n    * @param values sequence of values to tokenize\n    * @return iterator of tokens\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Tokenizer Implementations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and bytes types.\n  *\n  * @param encoding character encoding for bytes (default: UTF-8)\n  * @param normalizer optional function to normalize strings\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n  normalizer: Option[String => String] = None\n) extends BaseTokenizer[String] {\n\n  private val normalize = normalizer.getOrElse(identity[String])\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a string value.\n    *\n    * @param value the string to tokenize\n    * @return Token with STRING type\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: String): Token = {\n    val normalized = normalize(value)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize bytes after decoding.\n    *\n    * @param bytes the bytes to tokenize\n    * @return Token with STRING type\n    */\n  def tokenizeBytes(bytes: Array[Byte]): Token = {\n    val str = new String(bytes, encoding)\n    tokenize(str)\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for numeric types with precision handling.\n  *\n  * @param precision decimal places for floating point numbers\n  * @param formatOptions additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Number] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a numeric value.\n    *\n    * @param value the number to tokenize\n    * @return Token with NUMERIC type and metadata\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: Number): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case i: java.lang.Integer => i.toString\n      case l: java.lang.Long => l.toString\n      case f: java.lang.Float => f\"${f.doubleValue()}%.${precision}f\"\n      case d: java.lang.Double => f\"$d%.${precision}f\"\n      case bd: java.math.BigDecimal => f\"${bd.doubleValue()}%.${precision}f\"\n      case _ => value.toString\n    }\n\n    Token(\n      strValue,\n      TokenType.NUMERIC,\n      Map(\"original_type\" -> value.getClass.getSimpleName) ++ formatOptions\n    )\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatStr optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatStr: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[java.time.temporal.Temporal] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a date or time value.\n    *\n    * @param value the date/time to tokenize\n    * @return Token with TEMPORAL type\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: java.time.temporal.Temporal): Token = {\n    val str = value match {\n      case ldt: LocalDateTime => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss\")\n      case ld: LocalDate => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd\")\n      case _ => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd\")\n    }\n\n    val formatter = java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(str)\n    Token(formatter.format(value), TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer with Pattern Matching\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Universal tokenizer that handles multiple types through pattern matching.\n  * Uses type classes internally for extensibility.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new NumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize any value by dispatching to appropriate tokenizer.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return Token with appropriate type\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case Some(inner) => tokenize(inner)\n\n    case s: String => stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n    case b: Array[Byte] => stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(b)\n\n    case i: Int => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Int.box(i))\n    case l: Long => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Long.box(l))\n    case f: Float => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Float.box(f))\n    case d: Double => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Double.box(d))\n    case bd: java.math.BigDecimal => numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case ldt: LocalDateTime => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ldt)\n    case ld: LocalDate => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: convert to string\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.STRING, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a batch of values.\n    *\n    * @param values sequence of values to tokenize\n    * @return iterator of tokens\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[Any]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Tokenization Support\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Sealed hierarchy for JSON values enabling exhaustive pattern matching.\n  */\nsealed trait JsonValue\n\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonBoolean(value: Boolean) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Vector[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(fields: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * @param pretty format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Convert JsonValue to string representation.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to serialize\n    * @return JSON string\n    */\n  private def jsonToString(json: JsonValue): String = json match {\n    case JsonNull => \"null\"\n    case JsonBoolean(b) => b.toString\n    case JsonString(s) => s\"\"\"\"${escape(s)}\"\"\"\"\n    case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n    case JsonArray(vals) =>\n      vals.map(jsonToString).mkString(\"[\", \", \", \"]\")\n    case JsonObject(fields) =>\n      fields.map { case (k, v) =>\n        s\"\"\"\"${escape(k)}\": ${jsonToString(v)}\"\"\"\n      }.mkString(\"{\", \", \", \"}\")\n  }\n\n  private def escape(s: String): String =\n    s.replace(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(\"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to tokenize\n    * @return Token with STRUCTURED type\n    */\n  def tokenize(json: JsonValue): Token = {\n    val str = jsonToString(json)\n    Token(\n      str,\n      TokenType.STRUCTURED,\n      Map(\"format\" -> \"json\", \"pretty\" -> pretty)\n    )\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path (dot-separated).\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to traverse\n    * @param path dot-separated path (e.g., \"user.name\")\n    * @return Option containing Token if path is valid\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(json: JsonValue, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    var current: JsonValue = json\n\n    for (part <- parts) {\n      current = current match {\n        case JsonObject(fields) =>\n          fields.getOrElse(part, return None)\n        case JsonArray(vals) =>\n          Try(part.toInt).toOption\n            .flatMap(idx => if (idx >= 0 && idx < vals.length) Some(vals(idx)) else None)\n            .getOrElse(return None)\n        case _ => return None\n      }\n    }\n\n    Some(tokenize(current))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Basic tokenizer that splits text by whitespace.\n  *\n  * This is a fundamental tokenization operation used in NLP and text processing.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase convert all tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength minimum token length (shorter tokens filtered out)\n  * @param maxLength maximum token length (longer tokens truncated)\n  * @param stripPunctuation remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".\", \",\", \"!\", \"?\", \";\", \":\", \"'\", \"\\\"\", \"(\", \")\", \"[\", \"]\", \"{\", \"}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into a token string or None if filtered.\n    *\n    * @param word the word to process\n    * @return Option containing processed word or None if filtered\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      processed = processed\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else {\n      maxLength match {\n        case Some(max) => Some(processed.take(max))\n        case None => Some(processed)\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of Token objects\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): Vector[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap { case (word, position) =>\n      processToken(word).map { processed =>\n        Token(\n          processed,\n          TokenType.STRING,\n          Map(\"position\" -> position, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }.toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): Vector[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): Vector[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n      .foldLeft((Vector.empty[(String, Int, Int)], 0)) { case ((acc, currentPos), word) =>\n        val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n        val end = start + word.length\n\n        val result = processToken(word) match {\n          case Some(processed) => acc :+ (processed, start, end)\n          case None => acc\n        }\n\n        (result, end)\n      }\n      ._1\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text input text\n    * @return token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int =\n    tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Advanced Functional Constructs\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry for managing multiple tokenizers and their handlers.\n  * Demonstrates complex nested generic types.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the type of items being tokenized\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private val registry = scala.collection.mutable.Map.empty[String, TokenContainer[T]]\n  private val handlers = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer.empty[T => Option[Token]]\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items.\n    *\n    * @param key identifier for the container\n    * @param container the TokenContainer to register\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    registry(key) = container\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function.\n    *\n    * @param handler function that may produce a Token\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    handlers += handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * @param key identifier of the container to process\n    * @return vector of Options containing tokens\n    */\n  def process(key: String): Vector[Option[Token]] = {\n    registry.get(key).map { container =>\n      container.getAll.map { item =>\n        handlers\n          .map(_(item))\n          .collectFirst { case Some(token) => token }\n      }\n    }.getOrElse(Vector.empty)\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Functor for tokens supporting map and flatMap operations.\n  *\n  * @param value the wrapped value\n  * @tparam T the type of the wrapped value\n  */\nclass TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Map a function over the wrapped value.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return new TokenFunctor with transformed value\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] =\n    new TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * FlatMap a function returning TokenFunctor.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return result of applying the function\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): TokenFunctor[U] =\n    func(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Get the wrapped value or provide a default.\n    *\n    * @param default value if wrapped value is None\n    * @return the wrapped value or default\n    */\n  def getOrElse(default: T): T =\n    if (value == null) default else value\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad extending TokenFunctor with additional operations.\n  *\n  * @param value the wrapped value\n  * @tparam T the type of the wrapped value\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a wrapped function to this functor.\n    *\n    * @param funcWrapped wrapped function\n    * @return result of application\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcWrapped: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] = ???\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  /**\n    * Wrap a value in a TokenMonad.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to wrap\n    * @return new TokenMonad containing the value\n    */\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] =\n    new TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates the builder pattern with method chaining.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the input type being tokenized\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T] {\n  private var normalizers: List[String => String] = List.empty\n  private var validators: List[T => Boolean] = List.empty\n  private var builderMetadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Add a string normalizer function.\n    *\n    * @param normalizer function to normalize token strings\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): this.type = {\n    normalizers = normalizers :+ normalizer\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a validator function.\n    *\n    * @param validator function that returns true if valid\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): this.type = {\n    validators = validators :+ validator\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add metadata to all produced tokens.\n    *\n    * @param pairs metadata key-value pairs\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): this.type = {\n    builderMetadata = builderMetadata ++ pairs\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    *\n    * @return function that tokenizes values\n    */\n  def build: T => Either[String, Token] = { value =>\n    // Validate first\n    val validationError = ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Type class for converting values to tokens.\n  * Enables ad-hoc polymorphism for arbitrary types.\n  *\n  * @tparam A the type that can be tokenized\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable[A] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a value to a Token.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return the resulting Token\n    */\n  def toToken(value: A): Token\n}\n\nobject Tokenizable {\n  /**\n    * Summon an implicit Tokenizable instance for type A.\n    *\n    * @tparam A the type to tokenize\n    * @return the Trait\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract base tokenizer with generic input type.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the input type to tokenize\n  */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return the resulting Token\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and bytes types.\n  *\n  * @param encoding character encoding for bytes (default: UTF-8)\n  * @param normalizer optional function to normalize strings\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n  encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n  normalizer: Option[String => String] = None\n) extends additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Number] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a numeric value.\n    *\n    * @param value the number to tokenize\n    * @return Token with NUMERIC type and metadata\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: Number): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case i: java.lang.Integer => i.toString\n      case l: java.lang.Long => l.toString\n      case f: java.lang.Float => f\"${f.doubleValue()}%.${precision}f\"\n      case d: java.lang.Double => f\"$d%.${precision}f\"\n      case bd: java.math.BigDecimal => f\"${bd.doubleValue()}%.${precision}f\"\n      case _ => value.toString\n    }\n\n    Token(\n      strValue,\n      TokenType.NUMERIC,\n      Map(\"original_type\" -> value.getClass.getSimpleName) ++ formatOptions\n    )\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types.\n  *\n  * @param formatStr optional custom format string\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatStr: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[java.time.temporal.Temporal] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a date or time value.\n    *\n    * @param value the date/time to tokenize\n    * @return Token with TEMPORAL type\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: java.time.temporal.Temporal): Token = {\n    val str = value match {\n      case ldt: LocalDateTime => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss\")\n      case ld: LocalDate => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd\")\n      case _ => formatStr.getOrElse(\"yyyy-MM-dd\")\n    }\n\n    val formatter = java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(str)\n    Token(formatter.format(value), TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer with Pattern Matching\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Universal tokenizer that handles multiple types through pattern matching.\n  * Uses type classes internally for extensibility.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new NumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize any value by dispatching to appropriate tokenizer.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return Token with appropriate type\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case Some(inner) => tokenize(inner)\n\n    case s: String => stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n    case b: Array[Byte] => stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(b)\n\n    case i: Int => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Int.box(i))\n    case l: Long => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Long.box(l))\n    case f: Float => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Float.box(f))\n    case d: Double => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Double.box(d))\n    case bd: java.math.BigDecimal => numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case ldt: LocalDateTime => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ldt)\n    case ld: LocalDate => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: convert to string\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.STRING, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a batch of values.\n    *\n    * @param values sequence of values to tokenize\n    * @return iterator of tokens\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[Any]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Tokenization Support\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Sealed hierarchy for JSON values enabling exhaustive pattern matching.\n  */\nsealed trait JsonValue\n\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonBoolean(value: Boolean) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Vector[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(fields: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * @param pretty format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Convert JsonValue to string representation.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to serialize\n    * @return JSON string\n    */\n  private def jsonToString(json: JsonValue): String = json match {\n    case JsonNull => \"null\"\n    case JsonBoolean(b) => b.toString\n    case JsonString(s) => s\"\"\"\"${escape(s)}\"\"\"\"\n    case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n    case JsonArray(vals) =>\n      vals.map(jsonToString).mkString(\"[\", \", \", \"]\")\n    case JsonObject(fields) =>\n      fields.map { case (k, v) =>\n        s\"\"\"\"${escape(k)}\": ${jsonToString(v)}\"\"\"\n      }.mkString(\"{\", \", \", \"}\")\n  }\n\n  private def escape(s: String): String =\n    s.replace(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(\"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to tokenize\n    * @return Token with STRUCTURED type\n    */\n  def tokenize(json: JsonValue): Token = {\n    val str = jsonToString(json)\n    Token(\n      str,\n      TokenType.STRUCTURED,\n      Map(\"format\" -> \"json\", \"pretty\" -> pretty)\n    )\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path (dot-separated).\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to traverse\n    * @param path dot-separated path (e.g., \"user.name\")\n    * @return Option containing Token if path is valid\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(json: JsonValue, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    var current: JsonValue = json\n\n    for (part <- parts) {\n      current = current match {\n        case JsonObject(fields) =>\n          fields.getOrElse(part, return None)\n        case JsonArray(vals) =>\n          Try(part.toInt).toOption\n            .flatMap(idx => if (idx >= 0 && idx < vals.length) Some(vals(idx)) else None)\n            .getOrElse(return None)\n        case _ => return None\n      }\n    }\n\n    Some(tokenize(current))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Basic tokenizer that splits text by whitespace.\n  *\n  * This is a fundamental tokenization operation used in NLP and text processing.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase convert all tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength minimum token length (shorter tokens filtered out)\n  * @param maxLength maximum token length (longer tokens truncated)\n  * @param stripPunctuation remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".\", \",\", \"!\", \"?\", \";\", \":\", \"'\", \"\\\"\", \"(\", \")\", \"[\", \"]\", \"{\", \"}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into a token string or None if filtered.\n    *\n    * @param word the word to process\n    * @return Option containing processed word or None if filtered\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      processed = processed\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else {\n      maxLength match {\n        case Some(max) => Some(processed.take(max))\n        case None => Some(processed)\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of Token objects\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): Vector[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap { case (word, position) =>\n      processToken(word).map { processed =>\n        Token(\n          processed,\n          TokenType.STRING,\n          Map(\"position\" -> position, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }.toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): Vector[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): Vector[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n      .foldLeft((Vector.empty[(String, Int, Int)], 0)) { case ((acc, currentPos), word) =>\n        val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n        val end = start + word.length\n\n        val result = processToken(word) match {\n          case Some(processed) => acc :+ (processed, start, end)\n          case None => acc\n        }\n\n        (result, end)\n      }\n      ._1\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text input text\n    * @return token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int =\n    tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Advanced Functional Constructs\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry for managing multiple tokenizers and their handlers.\n  * Demonstrates complex nested generic types.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the type of items being tokenized\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private val registry = scala.collection.mutable.Map.empty[String, TokenContainer[T]]\n  private val handlers = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer.empty[T => Option[Token]]\n\n  /**\n    * Register a container of items.\n    *\n    * @param key identifier for the container\n    * @param container the TokenContainer to register\n    */\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    registry(key) = container\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a handler function.\n    *\n    * @param handler function that may produce a Token\n    */\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    handlers += handler\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Process all items in a container through all handlers.\n    *\n    * @param key identifier of the container to process\n    * @return vector of Options containing tokens\n    */\n  def process(key: String): Vector[Option[Token]] = {\n    registry.get(key).map { container =>\n      container.getAll.map { item =>\n        handlers\n          .map(_(item))\n          .collectFirst { case Some(token) => token }\n      }\n    }.getOrElse(Vector.empty)\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Functor for tokens supporting map and flatMap operations.\n  *\n  * @param value the wrapped value\n  * @tparam T the type of the wrapped value\n  */\nclass TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Map a function over the wrapped value.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return new TokenFunctor with transformed value\n    */\n  def map[U](func: T => U): TokenFunctor[U] =\n    new TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  /**\n    * FlatMap a function returning TokenFunctor.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return result of applying the function\n    */\n  def flatMap[U](func: T => TokenFunctor[U]): TokenFunctor[U] =\n    func(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Get the wrapped value or provide a default.\n    *\n    * @param default value if wrapped value is None\n    * @return the wrapped value or default\n    */\n  def getOrElse(default: T): T =\n    if (value == null) default else value\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad extending TokenFunctor with additional operations.\n  *\n  * @param value the wrapped value\n  * @tparam T the type of the wrapped value\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  /**\n    * Apply a wrapped function to this functor.\n    *\n    * @param funcWrapped wrapped function\n    * @return result of application\n    */\n  def ap[U](funcWrapped: TokenMonad[T => U]): TokenMonad[U] = ???\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  /**\n    * Wrap a value in a TokenMonad.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to wrap\n    * @return new TokenMonad containing the value\n    */\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] =\n    new TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  *\n  * Demonstrates the builder pattern with method chaining.\n  *\n  * @tparam T the input type being tokenized\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T] {\n  private var normalizers: List[String => String] = List.empty\n  private var validators: List[T => Boolean] = List.empty\n  private var builderMetadata: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n\n  /**\n    * Add a string normalizer function.\n    *\n    * @param normalizer function to normalize token strings\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): this.type = {\n    normalizers = normalizers :+ normalizer\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add a validator function.\n    *\n    * @param validator function that returns true if valid\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): this.type = {\n    validators = validators :+ validator\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Add metadata to all produced tokens.\n    *\n    * @param pairs metadata key-value pairs\n    * @return this builder for chaining\n    */\n  def withMetadata(pairs: (String, Any)*): this.type = {\n    builderMetadata = builderMetadata ++ pairs\n    this\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    *\n    * @return function that tokenizes values\n    */\n  def build: T => Either[String, Token] = { value =>\n    // Validate first\n    val validationError = validators.find(!_(value))\n      .map(_ => s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n\n    validationError match {\n      case Some(error) => Left(error)\n      case None =>\n        val strValue = * @param items sequence of items to store\n  * @tparam T the type of items (covariant)\n  */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  /**\n @return current value\n    */\n  def get: T = value\n\n  /**\n    * Set a new value.\n    *\n    * @param newValue the value to set\n    */\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = {\n    value = newValue\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Transform the current value using a function.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return the new value after transformation\n    */\n  def transform(func: T => */\ntrait BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  /**\n    * Convert a single value to a token.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return the resulting Token\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token with STRING type\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: String): Token = {\n    val normalized = normalize(value)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize bytes additional formatting options\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n  precision: Int = 6,\n  formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map.empty\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Number] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a numeric value.\n    *\n    * @param value the number to tokenize\n    * @return Token with NUMERIC type and metadata\n    */\n  override def tokenize(value: Number): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(formatStr: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[java.time.temporal.Temporal] {\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a date or time value.\n    *\n    * @param value the formatter = java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(str)\n    Token(formatter.format(value), TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Universal Tokenizer with Pattern Matching\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Universal tokenizer that handles multiple types through pattern matching.\n  * Uses type classes internally for extensibility.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new NumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize any value by dispatching to appropriate tokenizer.\n    *\n    * @param value the value to tokenize\n    * @return Token with appropriate type\n    */\n  def tokenize(value: Any): Token = value match {\n    case null => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    case Some(inner) => tokenize(inner)\n\n    case s: String => stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n    case b: Array[Byte] => stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(b)\n\n    case i: Int => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Int.box(i))\n    case l: Long => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Long.box(l))\n    case f: Float => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Float.box(f))\n    case d: Double => numericTokenizer.tokenize(Double.box(d))\n    case bd: java.math.BigDecimal => numericTokenizer.tokenize(bd)\n\n    case ldt: LocalDateTime => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ldt)\n    case ld: LocalDate => temporalTokenizer.tokenize(ld)\n\n    case other =>\n      // Fallback: convert to string\n      Token(other.toString, TokenType.STRING, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a batch of values.\n    *\n    * @param values sequence of values to tokenize\n    * @return iterator of tokens\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[Any]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Tokenization Support\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Sealed hierarchy for JSON values enabling exhaustive pattern matching.\n  */\nsealed trait JsonValue\n\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonBoolean(value: Boolean) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Vector[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(fields: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive type support.\n  *\n  * @param pretty format JSON with indentation\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n\n  /**\n    * Convert JsonValue to string representation.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to serialize\n    * @return JSON string\n    */\n  private def jsonToString(json: JsonValue): String = json match {\n    case JsonNull => \"null\"\n    case JsonBoolean(b) => b.toString\n    case JsonString(s) => s\"\"\"\"${escape(s)}\"\"\"\"\n    case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n    case JsonArray(vals) =>\n      vals.map(jsonToString).mkString(\"[\", \", \", \"]\")\n    case JsonObject(fields) =>\n      fields.map { case (k, v) =>\n        s\"\"\"\"${escape(k)}\": ${jsonToString(v)}\"\"\"\n      }.mkString(\"{\", \", \", \"}\")\n  }\n\n  private def escape(s: String): String =\n    s.replace(\"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(\"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize a JSON value.\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to tokenize\n    * @return Token with STRUCTURED type\n    */\n  def tokenize(json: JsonValue): Token = {\n    val str = jsonToString(json)\n    Token(\n      str,\n      TokenType.STRUCTURED,\n      Map(\"format\" -> \"json\", \"pretty\" -> pretty)\n    )\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path (dot-separated).\n    *\n    * @param json the JsonValue to traverse\n    * @param path dot-separated path (e.g., \"user.name\")\n    * @return Option containing Token if path is valid\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(json: JsonValue, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    var current: JsonValue = json\n\n    for (part <- parts) {\n      current = current match {\n        case JsonObject(fields) =>\n          fields.getOrElse(part, return None)\n        case JsonArray(vals) =>\n          Try(part.toInt).toOption\n            .flatMap(idx => if (idx >= 0 && idx < vals.length) Some(vals(idx)) else None)\n            .getOrElse(return None)\n        case _ => return None\n      }\n    }\n\n    Some(tokenize(current))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Basic tokenizer that splits text by whitespace.\n  *\n  * This is a fundamental tokenization operation used in NLP and text processing.\n  *\n  * @param lowercase convert all tokens to lowercase\n  * @param minLength minimum token length (shorter tokens filtered out)\n  * @param maxLength maximum token length (longer tokens truncated)\n  * @param stripPunctuation remove leading/trailing punctuation\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n  lowercase: Boolean = false,\n  minLength: Int = 0,\n  maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n  stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) {\n  private val punctuation = Set(\".\", \",\", \"!\", \"?\", \";\", \":\", \"'\", \"\\\"\", \"(\", \")\", \"[\", \"]\", \"{\", \"}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into a token string or None if filtered.\n    *\n    * @param word the word to process\n    * @return Option containing processed word or None if filtered\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      processed = processed\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n        .dropWhile(c => punctuation.contains(c.toString))\n        .reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else {\n      maxLength match {\n        case Some(max) => Some(processed.take(max))\n        case None => Some(processed)\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of Token objects\n    */\n  def tokenize(text: String): Vector[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap { case (word, position) =>\n      processToken(word).map { processed =>\n        Token(\n          processed,\n          TokenType.STRING,\n          Map(\"position\" -> position, \"original\" -> word)\n        )\n      }\n    }.toVector\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of token strings.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of token strings\n    */\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): Vector[String] =\n    tokenize(text).map(_.value)\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize text and return tokens with character positions.\n    *\n    * @param text input text to tokenize\n    * @return list of tuples (token_string, start_pos, end_pos)\n    */\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): Vector[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n      .foldLeft((Vector.empty[(String, Int, Int)], 0)) { case ((acc, currentPos), word) =>\n        val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n        val end = start + word.length\n\n        val result = processToken(word) match {\n          case Some(processed) => acc :+ (processed, start, end)\n          case None => acc\n        }\n\n        (result, end)\n      }\n      ._1\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Return the number of tokens in the text.\n    *\n    * @param text input text\n    * @return token count\n    */\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int =\n    tokenize(text).length\n}\n\n// registry.get(key).map { container =>\n      container.getAll.map { item =>\n        handlers\n          .map(_(item))\n          .collectFirst { case Some(token) => token }\n      }\n    }.getOrElse(Vector.empty)\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Functor for tokens supporting map and flatMap operations.\n  *\n  * @param value the wrapped value\n  * @tparam T the type of the wrapped value\n  */\nclass TokenFunctor[T](value: T) {\n  /**\n    * Map a function over the wrapped value.\n    *\n    * @param func transformation function\n    * @return new TokenFunctor with transformed value\n
